Battle-tested within Alibaba Group, it delivers production-grade, low-latency and scalable similarity search with minimal setup.\n\n> [!Important]\n> 🚀 **v0.5.0 (June 12, 2026)**\n>\n> - **Full-Text Search (FTS)**: Native full-text search — attach an FTS index to any string field and query it with natural-language or structured expressions, no external search engine required.\n> - **Hybrid Retrieval**: Combine full-text, dense-vector, and sparse-vector retrieval in a single query call, with scalar filtering and reranking.\n> - **DiskANN Index**: New on-disk index that keeps the bulk of the index on disk, drastically cutting memory usage for large-scale datasets.\n> - **Ecosystem & Platforms**: New official [Go](https://github.com/zvec-ai/zvec-go) / [Rust](https://github.com/zvec-ai/zvec-rust) SDKs, the [Zvec Studio](https://github.com/zvec-ai/zvec-studio) visual tool, and RISC-V support.\n>\n> 👉 [Read the Release Notes](https://github.com/alibaba/zvec/releases/tag/v0.5.0) | [View Roadmap 📍](https://github.com/alibaba/zvec/issues/309)\n\n## 💫 Features\n\n- **Blazing Fast**: Searches billions of vectors in milliseconds.\n- **Simple, Just Works**: [Install](#-installation) and start searching in seconds. Pure local, no servers, no config, no fuss.\n- **Dense + Sparse Vectors**: Support dense and sparse embeddings, multi-vector queries, and a rich selection of [vector index types](https://zvec.org/en/docs/db/concepts/vector-index/#vector-index-types) that scale from memory to disk.\n- **Full-Text Search (FTS)**: Native keyword-based full-text search — query string fields with natural-language or structured expressions.\n- **Hybrid Search**: Fuse vector similarity, full-text search, and structured filters in a single query for precise results.\n- **Durable Storage**: Write-ahead logging (WAL) guarantees persistence — data is never lost, even on process crash or power failure.\n- **Concurrent Access**: Multiple processes can read the same collection simultaneously; That dump should persist the writing segment data. Then it adds to segment_manager_ and the version.\n\nBut wait — the scenario says the recovered documents are missing after optimize. So the recovered writing segment must have `doc_count() == 0` from the perspective of the in-memory state, OR the dump path doesn't persist the recovered data.\n\nKey: after recovery, the writing segment is opened with `Segment::Open(...)` using the persisted `writing_segment_meta`. The recovered docs come from WAL replay. So the writing segment's in-memory indexers have the docs, but the segment meta (which is what gets persisted in the version) may not reflect them — i.e., the meta's doc count / forward block info is stale (from the last flush before the crash).\n\nThen in `Optimize`, `writing_segment_->doc_count()` — hmm, if doc_count() returns non-zero, it would call switch_to_new_segment_for_writing → dump. Let's check what `dump()` does vs `flush()`.\n\nActually wait. Let's think about the scenario: \"opening the collection recovers the documents, and they can be counted and fetched. Call sequence + exact condition**\n\n`Collection::Optimize` (collection.cc:779-915):\n- 794: `if (writing_segment_->doc_count() != 0)` — true, because recovered docs are in `doc_ids_`.\n- 796: `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing()` (1517-1552):\n  - 1519 `writing_segment_->dump()` → `SegmentImpl::dump()` (2197-2215) → `flush()` (2201).\n  - **`flush()` returns `Status::OK()` at segment.cc:2220-2222 because `wal_file_ == nullptr`.** This is the exact condition. Everything that would have made the data durable is skipped: `memory_store_->flush()` (2233-2236), `finish_memory_components()` (2288-2291 → 4342-4412, which is what calls `segment_meta_->add_persisted_block(...)` at 4368/4383/4401 and moves `memory_vector_indexers_` into `vector_indexers_`), `update_version()` (2301 → 4414-4426), and WAL removal (2305-2315).\n  - `dump()` still returns OK (it just seals invert/FTS indexers, 2204-2212).\n  - 1522 the un-persisted segment is registered as a *persist* segment in `segment_manager_`.\n  - 1537-1538 `writing_segment_meta = writing_segment_->meta()` (the same `shared_ptr` as `segment_meta_`, segment.cc:585-587) then `remove_writing_forward_block()` (meta.h:263-265) — this deletes the only on-disk pointer to the WAL.\n  - 1539 `version.add_persisted_segment_meta(...)`, 1546 `apply`, **1548 `version_manager_->flush()`** → `Version::Save` (version_manager.cc:77-116) writes `manifest.<n>` and deletes `manifest.<n-1>` (282-301). Point of no return: the manifest now describes segment 0 as a persisted segment with **zero blocks** and no writing block.\n- 802-803 `persist_segments` = [seg0]; 807 not empty.\n- 813-819 `build_compact_task` → with one segment and `rebuild == false` (931-935, no deletes) the trailing branch 991-1002 emits a `CreateVectorIndexTask`; `execute_compact_task` → `SegmentHelper::ExecuteCreateVectorIndexTask` (segment_helper.cc:1113-1125) → `create_all_vector_index` (segment.cc:1617-1640), which copies `*segment_meta_` and again `remove_writing_forward_block()` (1624-1625). For the default FLAT params (`DefaultVectorIndexParams`, index_params.h:437; marked indexed at Create, segment.cc:534-539) `create_vector_index` short-circuits at 1685-1690 → OK.\n- 827-877: `update_persisted_segment_meta(output_segment_meta_)` (865-867) then `apply` + `flush` → manifest.<n+1>, still with no forward block for seg0.\n- 880-911: `reload_vector_index` (segment.cc:1882-1940) swaps `segment_meta_` to the output meta but leaves `doc_ids_`, `memory_store_`, `memory_vector_indexers_` untouched.\n- 914 `return Status::OK()`.\n\nOn the next open: `recovery()` (collection.cc:1858-1874) opens seg0 read-only; `SegmentImpl::Open` finds no `writing_forward_block` (segment.cc:484-490) so `recover()` is never called and the still-present `<path>/0/0.wal` is never read (the file isn't even deleted — `cleanup()` runs only if `destroy()` was called, segment.cc:112-117, 2326-2337, and Optimize only destroys CompactTask inputs, collection.cc:898-901). `load_persist_scalar_blocks` finds no SCALAR block → `doc_ids_` empty → doc_count 0.\n\nControl case: same `Optimize`, but `wal_file_ != nullptr && has_record()` so `flush()` runs the full body; `finish_memory_components()` adds the SCALAR/VECTOR_INDEX BlockMetas and `update_version()` persists them before `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing` removes the writing block. Hence no loss.\n\nAlso worth noting: the same no-op flush makes `Collection::Flush()` (collection.cc:380-395) and the `Close()` flush (340-349) silently ineffective on a recovery-only handle; that is harmless *by itself* because the manifest still points at the WAL. It becomes data loss only when a manifest rewrite drops that pointer. Other callers of `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing` share the hazard: `CreateIndex` (502), `DropIndex` (675), `AddColumn` (1155), `DropColumn` (1227), `AlterColumn` (1311), and the size-driven rollover in `write_impl` (1445-1448) — though the last one is preceded by a real write, so `wal_file_` is set by then.\n\nHmm, careful: in `write_impl`, `need_switch_to_new_segment()` is checked BEFORE the write, so if the recovered segment is already at max_doc_count, the switch happens before any `append_wal` in this process → same bug. But the prompt excludes rollover. I'll mention briefly.\n\n**3. Why success + stats ≠ durability**\n\n- `Status::OK()` from `flush()` is returned by the early-out at 2220-2222 without any I/O; OK means \"nothing to do\", not \"bytes are on disk\". `dump()` propagates that OK (2201-2214). No caller distinguishes the two.\n- `Stats()` (collection.cc:403-442) sums `segment->doc_count(delete_store_->make_filter())`, and `doc_count()` is `doc_ids_.size()` (segment.cc:589-599) — a purely in-memory vector that Optimize never clears. `index_completeness` reads `segment->meta()->vector_indexed(...)`, which `create_all_vector_index` sets on the output meta (1636). Both are satisfied by RAM state.\n- Nothing in the read path cross-checks the manifest/BlockMeta against `doc_ids_`. In fact the divergence is observable in-process: after the switch, `SegmentMeta::max_doc_id()` returns 0 (meta.h:293-305, no writing block, no SCALAR persisted block), so `SegmentImpl::Fetch` rejects every `g_doc_id > 0` at segment.cc:1102 — while `Stats()` still reports N docs. Durability here is a property of the manifest + block files, and the only trustworthy check is a reopen in a fresh process (or an assertion that every in-memory doc range is covered by a persisted BlockMeta).\n\n**4. Smallest repair principle + regression test**\n\nRepair principle (describe, don't implement): make \"there is un-persisted recovered state\" a first-class condition instead of inferring it from `wal_file_`. Minimal options, in increasing scope:\n\n(a) Smallest: in `SegmentImpl::recover()`, after a successful replay of ≥1 record, leave the WAL open for append in the member `wal_file_` (i.e., call `open_wal_file()` / assign the handle it already created) instead of closing the local `recover_wal_file`. Then `has_record()` is true and the existing `flush()` body runs unchanged. This makes the recovery path converge on the ordinary insertion path with a one-line-ish change and no new state.\n   - Caveat to check when implementing: `LocalWalFile::open(create_new=false)` seeks to End (local_wal_file.cc:99-106) while `prepare_for_read()` seeks to Begin (143-158); replay uses a read cursor and later appends use the write offset, so either reuse one handle carefully (read to EOF, then append) or keep the replay handle separate and open the member handle after replay.\n\n(b) Equivalent and arguably more robust: change the `flush()` guard so it does not use `wal_file_` as the proxy for dirtiness. Gate on real dirty state — e.g. `memory_store_ != nullptr && memory_store_->num_rows() > 0`, or `segment_meta_->writing_forward_block()->doc_count_ != 0`, or `!doc_ids_.empty() && persisted blocks don't cover them` — and treat the WAL flush/remove steps as conditional (`if (wal_file_)`) rather than as the entry condition. segment.cc:2224-2229, 2305-2315 already null-check `wal_file_`, so only the guard at 2220-2222 needs to change.\n\n(c) Defense in depth at the caller: `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing()` (collection.cc:1517-1552) should not `remove_writing_forward_block()` + `add_persisted_segment_meta()` + `version_manager_->flush()` unless the dump actually produced a persisted SCALAR BlockMeta covering the segment's `doc_ids_` range. An invariant check there (\"a segment being promoted to persisted must have `meta()->doc_count() == doc_count()`\") turns silent loss into a loud error and protects all six callers.\n\nI'd recommend (b) as the true minimal fix (it fixes `Flush()`, `Close()`, `Optimize()` and every schema op at once), plus (c) as the invariant guard.\n\nRegression test (describe):\nLocation: `tests/db/crash_recovery/` — it already has the needed fixtures: `data_generator` (SIGKILL-able writer), `collection_optimizer` (open + `Optimize` + exit), `CreateTestSchema`/`CreateTestDoc` (`utility.h:38-156`), and `ExecuteProcess`/`ExecuteOptimizer` helpers (`write_recovery_test.cc:56-148`, `optimize_recovery_test.cc:53-128`). Note `schema->set_max_doc_count_per_segment(10000)` (utility.h:41) — keep the doc count well under that so no rollover, matching the reported scenario.\n\nSequence (new `TEST_F(CrashRecoveryTest, OptimizeAfterWalRecoveryWithoutNewWrites)`):\n1. `Collection::CreateAndOpen(dir_path_, schema, options_)`, then `collection.reset()` — clean empty collection (same as write_recovery_test.cc:183-189).\n2. `RunGeneratorAndCrash(\"0\", \"N\", \"insert\", \"0\", secs)` with N small (e.g. 500) — SIGKILL before `data_generator`'s `Flush()` (data_generator.cc:245-248), so the docs exist **only** in `<path>/0/0.wal`.\n3. `Collection::Open(dir_path_, options_)`; record `d1 = Stats().doc_count`; `ASSERT_GT(d1, 0)`; `Fetch` a sample of pks and compare to `CreateTestDoc(id, 0)`; run a `Query` and check hits. Optionally snapshot `d2 = Stats().doc_count` and assert `d2 == d1` (documents the \"same-handle stats look healthy\" half of the bug).\n5. `collection.reset()` (clean close).\n6. `Collection::Open(dir_path_, options_)` again in the same or a fresh process; `ASSERT_EQ(Stats().doc_count, d1)`; re-`Fetch` every pk in [0, d1) and `ASSERT_EQ(*actual, CreateTestDoc(id, 0))`; re-run the `Query` and assert the same top-k. **This assertion is the one that fails today** (it will observe 0 docs).\n7. Which internal state differs, and why the data is still readable\n\n**Ordinary insertion path**\n\n- `SegmentImpl::Insert` → `append_wal(doc)` → `internal_insert(doc)` — `segment.cc:903-918` (same shape for `Update` 920-936, `Upsert` 938-948, `Delete` 950-989).\n- `append_wal` lazily calls `open_wal_file()` — `segment.cc:4324-4340`, specifically 4327-4330.\n- `open_wal_file()` is the **only** place the member `wal_file_` is ever assigned — `segment.cc:4303-4322`, assignment at 4314; member declared at `segment.cc:401`.\n- So after any real write: `wal_file_ != nullptr` and `has_record()` is true (`file_.size() > sizeof(WalHeader)`, i.e. > 64 bytes — `src/db/index/storage/wal/local_wal_file.h:75-77`, header at 31-37).\n\n**Recovery path**\n\n- `CollectionImpl::Open` → `recovery()` — `src/db/collection.cc:308-329` and `1836-1892`; the writing segment is opened from the manifest's `writing_segment_meta` at `collection.cc:1879-1887`.\n- `SegmentImpl::Open` sees a `writing_forward_block` and calls `recover()` — `segment.cc:461-517`, gate at 484, call at 500.\n- `recover()` — `segment.cc:4193-4301` — opens a **local** `recover_wal_file` (4206-4214), replays each record through `internal_insert` / `internal_update` / `internal_upsert` / `internal_delete` (4250-4272), and closes it via `AILEGO_DEFER` (4215). **It never assigns the member `wal_file_`.**\n- `internal_insert` does *not* append to the WAL (contrast `Insert` at 914). It only populates RAM: `memory_store_->insert` (857), scalar/FTS/vector indexers (861-872), `doc_ids_.push_back` (878), and the in-memory block counters `mem_block.max_doc_id_` / `doc_count_` (874-876). `recover()` additionally advances `mem_block.max_doc_id_ += added_docs` at 4288.\n\n**The delta is therefore exactly one field:** after pure WAL recovery `wal_file_ == nullptr`, and the memory components are populated with **no corresponding persisted `BlockMeta`**.\n\n**Why it reads fine:** every read path is served from those RAM structures and never consults disk —\n- `doc_count()` is literally `doc_ids_.size()` — `segment.cc:589-599`;\n- forward fetch falls back to `memory_store_` — `segment.cc:2531-2532` and `2744-2756`;\n- `Stats()` sums `segment->doc_count(...)` over `get_all_segments()` — `collection.cc:403-442`, `2076-2086`.\n\nThe only on-disk evidence of the data is still `<collection>/<seg_id>/<block_id>.wal` (`src/db/common/file_helper.h:74-78`), reachable solely through the manifest's `writing_segment_meta.writing_forward_block`.\n\n---\n\n## 2. No loss.\n\n**Blast radius beyond `Optimize`:** the same no-op makes `Collection::Flush()` (`collection.cc:380-395`) and the `Close()` flush (`340-349`) silently ineffective on a recovery-only handle. That is *harmless by itself*, because the manifest still references the WAL and a later reopen replays it again. It becomes loss only when a manifest rewrite drops that reference — i.e. `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing`, which is also reached from `CreateIndex` (`collection.cc:502`), `DropIndex` (675), `AddColumn` (1155), `DropColumn` (1227), `AlterColumn` (1311), and the pre-write rollover in `write_impl` (1445-1448, excluded by your \"no rollover\" assumption).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Why `OK` + healthy stats prove nothing here\n\n- **`Status::OK()` is overloaded.** The early-out at `segment.cc:2220-2222` returns OK without touching the filesystem. `dump()` propagates it (2201-2214). No caller anywhere distinguishes \"nothing to persist\" from \"bytes are durable\".\n- **`Stats()` reads RAM only.** `collection.cc:421-423` sums `SegmentImpl::doc_count()` = `doc_ids_.size()` (`segment.cc:589-599`), a vector that nothing in the Optimize path clears. `index_completeness` (425-439) reads `meta()->vector_indexed(...)`, which `create_all_vector_index` *sets* on the output meta (`segment.cc:1636`). Both are satisfied entirely by memory state that the manifest no longer describes.\n- **The divergence is even observable in-process**, which shows stats are the wrong instrument: after the switch, `SegmentMeta::max_doc_id()` returns 0 (`meta.h:293-305` — no writing block, no SCALAR block), so `SegmentImpl::Fetch` rejects every `g_doc_id > 0` at `segment.cc:1102`, while `Stats()` still reports N documents.\n- Durability here is a property of *manifest + block files*. The only sound checks are (a) reopen in a fresh process, or (b) an invariant that every in-memory doc range is covered by a persisted `BlockMeta`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Smallest repair principle, and a regression test\n\n### Repair (described, not implemented)\n\nThe principle: **stop using the WAL handle as the proxy for \"this segment has un-persisted state\".** Recovered-but-unflushed is a real dirty state and must be treated as such.\n\n- **(b) Minimal and complete — fix the guard.** At `segment.cc:2220-2222`, gate on actual dirtiness instead of `wal_file_`: e.g. `memory_store_ != nullptr && memory_store_->num_rows() > 0`, or `writing_forward_block()->doc_count_ != 0`, or `!doc_ids_.empty()` not covered by persisted SCALAR blocks. The WAL-specific steps already null-check `wal_file_` individually (`2224-2229`, `2305-2315`), so only the entry condition changes. This one edit repairs `Flush()`, `Close()`, `Optimize()` and all five schema operations at once.\n- **(a) Alternative one-liner — converge the paths.** In `recover()` (`segment.cc:4193-4301`), when ≥1 record was replayed, leave the WAL open for append in the member `wal_file_` rather than closing the local `recover_wal_file` (4215). Then the existing `flush()` body runs unchanged. Implementation caveat: `LocalWalFile::open(create_new=false)` seeks to End (`local_wal_file.cc:99-106`) while `prepare_for_read()` seeks to Begin (`143-158`), so either read to EOF then append on one handle, or open the member handle after replay finishes.\n- **(c) Defence in depth at the caller.** `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing` (`collection.cc:1517-1552`) should refuse to `remove_writing_forward_block()` + `add_persisted_segment_meta()` + `version_manager_->flush()` unless the dump actually produced a persisted SCALAR `BlockMeta` covering the segment's `doc_ids_` — i.e. assert `meta()->doc_count() == doc_count()` before promoting a writing segment to persisted. This converts any future variant of silent loss into a loud error and protects all six call sites.\n\nRecommend (b) as the fix plus (c) as the invariant guard.\n\n### Regression test (described)\n\nPut it in `tests/db/crash_recovery/`, which already has every fixture needed: SIGKILL-able `data_generator` (`data_generator.cc`, whose clean path flushes at 245-248), `collection_optimizer` (`collection_optimizer.cc:107`), `CreateTestSchema`/`CreateTestDoc` (`utility.h:38-156`), and the process helpers `ExecuteProcess` (`write_recovery_test.cc:56-148`) / `ExecuteOptimizer` (`optimize_recovery_test.cc:53-128`). Keep N well under `set_max_doc_count_per_segment(10000)` (`utility.h:41`) so no rollover occurs, matching the report.\n\n`TEST_F(CrashRecoveryTest, OptimizeAfterWalRecoveryWithoutNewWrites)`:\n\n1. `Collection::CreateAndOpen(dir_path_, schema, options_)`, then `collection.reset()` — clean empty collection (pattern: `write_recovery_test.cc:183-189`).\n2. `RunGeneratorAndCrash(\"0\", \"500\", \"insert\", \"0\", secs)` — SIGKILL before the generator's `Flush()`, so the docs exist **only** in `<path>/0/0.wal`.\n3. `Collection::Open(dir_path_, options_)`; capture `d1 = Stats().doc_count`; `ASSERT_GT(d1, 0)` (use `GT`, not `EQ` — the kill is wall-clock based, cf. `write_recovery_test.cc:198`); `Fetch` a sample of pks and compare against `CreateTestDoc(id, 0)`; run a `Query` and record the top-k. This pins the \"recovered and readable\" state.\n4. **Insert nothing.** Call `collection->Optimize(OptimizeOptions{2})`; `ASSERT_TRUE(s.ok())`. Then capture `d2 = Stats().doc_count` and `ASSERT_EQ(d2, d1)` — this documents the \"same-handle stats still look healthy\" half of the bug.\n5. `collection.reset()` (clean close).\n6. `Collection::Open(dir_path_, options_)` again; `ASSERT_EQ(Stats().doc_count, d1)`; re-`Fetch` every pk in `[0, d1)` and `ASSERT_EQ(*actual, CreateTestDoc(id, 0))`; re-run the `Query` and compare the top-k. **Step 6 is the assertion that fails on the current tree** (it will observe 0 docs).\n7. Variants worth adding: call `Optimize` twice in a row (cf. `Feature_Optimize_Repeated`, `tests/db/collection_test.cc:2920`); substitute `CreateIndex` / `AddColumn` for `Optimize` in step 4, since they share `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing` (`collection.cc:502`, `1155`); and a control that inserts one doc after recovery before optimizing — that one passes today and locks in the non-crash behaviour.\n8. **Timing-free white-box companion** (no fork/kill, so not flaky): a unit test in `tests/db/` that, after WAL recovery, asserts the writing segment satisfies `meta()->doc_count() == doc_count()`, and that after `Flush()`/`Optimize()` its meta contains a persisted SCALAR `BlockMeta` whose `doc_count_` covers `doc_ids_`. That invariant is violated by the current code and is exactly guard (c).\n\n---\n\n## Source-established vs. needs-execution\n\n**Established by reading the tree:** the single assignment site of `wal_file_` (`segment.cc:4314`) and its only caller chain (`append_wal` ← `Insert/Update/Upsert/Delete`); `recover()`'s use of a separate local handle that it closes (`4206-4215`); the `flush()` guard (`2220-2222`) and everything it skips; the ordering in `switch_to_new_segment_for_writing` (`collection.cc:1519 → 1538 → 1548`); the manifest rewrite/rotate semantics (`version_manager.cc:282-301`); the reopen path that skips `recover()` when there is no writing block (`segment.cc:484-490`) and rebuilds `doc_ids_` only from SCALAR blocks (`3913-3957`); and that `Optimize` returns OK for both FLAT (`segment.cc:1685-1690`) and HNSW (`vector_column_indexer.cc:111-113`) index types.
